## 2026-11-06 Atomic prize writer/recovery and reciprocal guards — UNEXECUTED SOURCE

Added database/proposals/event-prize-transaction-source-v1.sql as a private, default-off candidate depending on the preceding guard/V3/V4 proposals. Both provider wrappers are revoked; no exposed grant is supplied. Source includes reciprocal BEFORE INSERT journal guards and the core carry trigger, but NONE has been executed or installed.

The writer takes both actor/operation advisory locks before tournament and current-role locks; exact historical replay precedes fresh source checks. Fresh operations cannot adopt either key from older journals. Reciprocal journal triggers take the same actor/key lock before checking collisions, including cross-tournament races; only the exact same-transaction nested V3/V4 chain may continue. Nested JSON rejection raises inside its savepoint; snapshot/binding/head staging rolls back together, and a bound rejected outcome can remain outside that block. Lock failure rolls back the outer function block. Current role rows are held FOR SHARE, including during read-only recovery.

Snapshot insert validation reconstructs raw plans and verifies actual prior head, reservation transaction/actor/digest, settings snapshot/reservation, nested operation, setup revision/version and false authority flags. Unknown historical prize versions fail closed. Recovery reads only owned immutable operations under current authorization, independently of current setup/head, and retains pending work on missing/uncertain outcomes. Existing old proposal files, codecs, drafts, layouts, Finance and mathematical policies are unchanged. This stores intent only; unresolved rounding and fee-comparison policy is not guessed.

The observed calculator rounds each suggested amount independently to $5, so rounded totals can differ from the pot although the unrounded linear formula conserves it. Its final Update validation was not tested. A separately versioned calculator policy is planned; director-visible balancing/confirmation remains an unresolved owner choice, not an ACC-mandated correction rule. Do not invent a general-account subsidy or enable unbalanced finalization. Existing accounting invariants remain source/version-bound. See `docs/reference/acc-side-pool-calculator-observation-2026-10-29.md` and `docs/quality/2026-10-29-acc-calculator-policy-v3-plan.md`.

## 2026-10-29 canonical unpaid Q bridge

Local Q review v2 and accounting v6 now preserve distinct base/Q funding, exact saved regular prizes and immutable unpaid liabilities. Finalization records accounting only; it does not move money or mark anybody paid. A later source correction may stale immutable final history but cannot automatically reopen or create another review/final liability. Existing v1/v5 history is not converted. New v2-policy events reject incompatible old-client v5 drafts before they can poison the new domain. Local verification and remaining release scope are in `docs/quality/2026-10-29-q-v2-canonical-accounting-local.md`. No publication/shared migration is implied. Separate qualification-to-playoff bridge verification is required because raw numeric ties intentionally differ from resolved seed order.

## 2026-10-29 qualification tie clarification

Owner approved showing original tied scorecards and recording a separately played one-game tiebreak winner without changing qualifying totals. Apply ACC 2025 Appendix A20 (printed55/PDF56) and Policy6IV.B.2: compare an unambiguous qualifying-round head-to-head first, otherwise one-game playoff with cut for first deal. First supported slice is exactly two tied players; repeated/ambiguous meetings and multiway ties stay blocked. Gross positive winning spreads means sum of positive winning margins, never net spread. Preserve independent Q-only monetary tie policy unchanged. See `docs/decisions/2026-10-29-qualification-two-player-ties.md`. Local implementation/testing is authorized; no hosted fixture changes or migration application is implied.

## 2026-10-29 owner clarification of six blurred Q chart rows

The owner approved specific readings for page-5 $10 / 1-in-4 paid counts 39 and 56–60. All six arrays conserve the pot and ceil(count/4) recipients, but are user-read clarifications, not independent visual extractions. Preserve row 56's qualified reading and the fourth-place ambiguity resolved by total in rows 57–59. The reusable catalog now supports 320 combinations within the supplied ranges; all other 314 amounts stay unchanged and outside-coverage combinations remain blocked. Preserve original PDF hash/page/version plus per-cell clarification provenance. Use `docs/reference/INDEX.md` for existing extracted notes/data; it explicitly does not represent full Director Manual extraction. No tie-policy or schema change is implied. See `docs/quality/2026-10-29-q-chart-six-row-clarification.md`.

## 2026-10-29 owner clarification: selectable friend-supplied Q schedules

Graduated Q selects ceil(current fully paid entrants in that named pool / selected ratio), descending qualifying-card order, and continues to paid nonqualifiers when needed. Equal Q selects only paid playoff qualifiers; no eligible qualifier creates unpaid contributor refund obligations. These are disclosed selectable operating policies, not a universal ACC formula. Use exact visually verified chart dollars; never scale fee tables or interpolate unsupported counts. Library source: `libfile_3a1e3f4477e481919c5163b368dcd5c2`, eight-page `cribbage pool info.pdf`. Supplied tie sequence is games won, positive plus points, then a physical low-card cut with recorded director outcome. Existing plus_points represents positive winning margins, not net spread. Q ordering must be separately versioned rather than silently changing the existing playoff qualification order, which includes net spread. Regular playoff semifinal losers share the combined third/fourth percentages equally. Preserve earlier immutable policies and unpaid review history. User approved implementation/test Preview publication; any necessary staging migration must be proposed and locally validated before separate application. Parent-supplied visually audited structured chart data resolved the failed PDF transfer dependency (314 supported cells; six blurred rows blocked). Scoped retries passed all local SQL/browser checks; tested commit be7d7e6 is on the test Preview and checksum-verified 0246 applied once to staging (history 20261029071013), with existing app data/unpaid history unchanged; see `docs/quality/2026-10-29-q-operating-schedules-progress.md`.

**Last updated:** 2026-09-20
**Purpose:** Preserve owner corrections and verified source facts across tasks
so a future worker does not depend on chat recall or ask the owner to repeat
recoverable information.

## Mandatory solve-first protocol

Before reporting a blocker or asking the owner what happens next:

1. Read this file, `PROJECT_STATUS.md`, the working outline, applicable
   requirements/decisions, and the relevant quality evidence.
2. Search the repository, cached source documents, tests, and existing
   implementation for the answer.
3. Inspect connected Vercel/Supabase state when relevant and authorized.
4. Try at least one safe, concrete, in-scope solution or diagnostic when one
   is available. Record the result.
5. Continue with any other independent in-scope work instead of stopping.
6. Ask the owner only when the remaining need is a genuine choice, approval,
   credential, physical-world test/action, or unavailable external fact.
   State exactly what was tried, what evidence failed, and the smallest action
   needed. Never stop without explaining the blocking condition.

Status reports must distinguish:

- **source available / implementation remaining**;
- **implemented / verification remaining**;
- **external approval or access required**; and
- **genuinely missing information**.

Do not call an item an information blocker merely because its code or
end-to-end proof is unfinished.

## Correction capture protocol

When the owner corrects or clarifies the project:

1. Add or amend the durable fact below with the date and provenance type
   (`Owner decision` or `ACC source`).
2. Reconcile `docs/product/requirements.md`, the applicable decision record,
   `docs/operations/WORKING_OUTLINE.md`, and tests when behavior changes.
3. Update `PROJECT_STATUS.md` and add quality evidence for material work.
4. If an owner statement conflicts with an official ACC source, preserve both,
   flag the conflict, and fail closed for official calculations until resolved.
5. Never silently overwrite a dated official rule or completed-tournament
   ruleset; create a new version.

## Genuine external dependencies

The worker must try all repository/source/service checks before escalating
these:

- ACC authorization for the app to replace or constitute an official digital
  operational record;
- an ACC-supported portal API/import boundary and credentials, if automated
  submission is desired;
- an authoritative current-effective schedule that cannot be established from
  the cached/public materials;
- real people/devices for final independent-user and physical pilot rehearsal;
- an owner/ACC policy choice where more than one safe interpretation remains.

These dependencies do not justify pausing unrelated implementation or tests.

## 2026-10-31: explicit fee safeguards (local, unpublished)

User confirmed paid Main minimum $3 and paid Consolation minimum $1 as APP safeguards, not ACC-mandated entry-price floors. New paid amounts must be explicitly supplied; blank, whitespace, missing/null, invalid, negative and zero never mean Free. Free events require an explicit choice. That choice does not waive sanctioning, prove subsidy or fabricate funding; existing accounting/funding reconciliation remains required. Enabled Q and Side Pool fees must be positive $5 increments (minimum $5); disabled pools need no fee. Optional expenses remain independent.

Source distinction: official Policy Manual Chapter6 I.E.10 printed6-2/PDF34 and IV.A.6.b printed6-4/PDF36 establish $3 Main/$1 Consolation sanction obligations. Optional Side Pools are distinct from Consolation events (Director Manual8.A–B pages12–13). No general retail minimum or blanket subsidy permission was established by these provisions. Numeric entry-price safeguards are Daron's product decision of 2026-10-31.

## Acceptance sequence clarified by Daron — November 1

Stage 1 is the single-operator, two-device TECHNICAL rehearsal. Daron operates both
fictional accounts. After separately approved staging rollout/enrollment and normal
setup, success means the two devices complete matching entries and per-account
confirmations, offline save/reload/reconnect and reconciliation with no duplicates
or lost results, with truthful simulation provenance throughout. A separate human
is NOT a prerequisite or pass criterion for this stage. Local automation is
supporting evidence; the deployed two-device exercise still needs to be run.

Only AFTER Stage 1 works will Daron recruit another person for Stage 2: whether
that person can understand and use the app, plus genuine independent-human identity
and scoring verification. Stage 1 passing is a valid technical result, not a failed
independence test. It does not by itself complete Stage 2 or production readiness.
Publication, hosted migration/enrollment/use and user-operated outage approvals
remain separate; this clarification performs none of those actions.

### Regular event payout setup and separate Finance — 2026-11-04 user direction
Replace the director-facing Main/Consolation regular declaration presentation with simple event payout settings: Payout Type choices Graduated,1-in-#(Custom),Top#(Custom), and editable incrementfactor default1.5, patterned on native MainCon Sheet. Do not delete immutable old declarations, source/review/audit or activation protection before the new versioned settings and disclosures replace them. Independent Sheet field mapping is underway; do not infer payout-recipient or tied-place semantics.
Finance is a separate section (not Setup): tournament details, player benefits, expenses, additional income, calculated payouts and actual payouts. Support extensible custom entries using stable IDs and append-only corrections, with no arbitrary business cap on the number of categories. Preserve technical batch/pagination safeguards. Estimates, documented actuals and simulated entries must be distinguishable. Regular event funds and optional Q/Side Pool funds remain separate; no automatic deduction of tournament expenses from optional pools. No real receipt/payment or hosted migration/publication authorized by this design direction. Custom N and playoff series checkpoints stay separate.

## 2026-11-11 Hawaii — Frozen Setup publication interruption and recovery rule

Owner request: remember this incident and prevent recurrence. Investigation restored normal GitHub CLI browser/device sign-in as chocolatebananamalt; credentials remain in the OS keyring. Never print or store token values.

Verified on 2026-11-11 Hawaii: remote chocolatebananamalt/Cribbage-web-app branch codex/director-setup-journey remains 2686815e8a28a7baf31beb08d9ffc81fa7161c34. All 216 frozen candidate files match manifest SHA-256 and byte size; candidate/base comparison contains exactly 173 additions and 43 modifications, no extra changed files. Twenty-four manifest paths differ in later local development: never publish the whole working tree or overwrite later work. First six blob SHAs in PUBLICATION-RESOLUTION.json exist on GitHub; all second-batch six SHAs remain unavailable. No release commit/ref update occurred. PR 122 has successful existing Verify checks; those checks do not validate the unpublished frozen candidate. This was NOT established as a GitHub CI failure.

Unknown root cause: original platform response for interrupted exec cell89 / second six-file upload batch was not retained. An OpenAI automatic-review interruption was reported, but exact denied action, reason and original execution state cannot be established from the surviving record. Do not invent a reason. The separately documented initial database denial (authorization interpreted as restore-only, later authorized retry succeeded) is resolved and must not be conflated with the upload interruption. Restored gh login does not resolve a platform review gate.

Required prevention and recovery procedure:
- Before a publication mutation, pin repository, branch, expected head, frozen manifest, reviewed diff and authorized scope. Preserve later development separately.
- Retain the exact tool request, review decision/reason, completion or interruption response, resulting object SHAs and readback outcome in a durable non-secret receipt after each completed batch. Do not rely on a transient exec cell as the only record. Never include credentials/private source in public receipts.
- After any interruption, reconcile remote objects and branch before retrying. Object existence is not publication; a 404 now does not prove the original review reason or that a request never ran.
- Recover the original platform review event where supported. If unavailable, explicitly report the missing evidence and obtain supported platform resolution; do not switch CLI/API/transport, rename files or split uploads to bypass a denial. Follow applicable retry policy and new relevant authorization only after the exact action/outcome is established.
- Once cleared, reuse verified first-six blobs, publish only frozen216, recheck expected-head lease, verify final tree/diff and PR, then CI and READY preview. PR 122's aggregate diff exceeds GitHub's 300-file diff endpoint limit: use supported paginated file inspection or an isolated local comparison; do not call a metadata-only PR check a full diff review.
- Do not reinstall successful staging migrations, reapply grants, activate participants/Finance, merge, promote or publish later development merely to finish this upload.

Evidence: test-results/native-mixed-finance-release-20261109/PUBLICATION-RESOLUTION.json, PUBLICATION-RESOLUTION.md, frozen-public-manifest.json; current investigation in chat Explain GitHub connection workflow. This note records a prevention procedure, not a guarantee that platform review will never interrupt future work. Publication remains paused pending supported resolution of the missing review event and full release diff review.
